### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Number of paramagnetic complexes among the following is ________.
$[\text{MnBr}_4]^{2-}$, $[\text{NiCl}_4]^{2-}$, $[\text{Ni(CN)}_4]^{2-}$, $[\text{Ni(CO)}_4]$, $[\text{CoF}_6]^{3-}$, $[\text{Fe(CN)}_6]^{4-}$, $[\text{Mn(CN)}_6]^{3-}$, $[\text{Ti(CN)}_6]^{3-}$, $[\text{Cu(H}_2\text{O)}_6]^{2+}$, $[\text{Co(C}_2\text{O}_4\text{)}_3]^{3-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

To determine the magnetic nature of the given complexes, we analyze the oxidation state, electronic configuration, and the nature of the ligands for each central metal atom/ion:

1. $[\text{MnBr}_4]^{2-}$: Mn$^{2+}$ has a $3d^5$ configuration. Br$^-$ is a weak field ligand. The complex is tetrahedral with $5$ unpaired electrons. (Paramagnetic)

2. $[\text{NiCl}_4]^{2-}$: Ni$^{2+}$ has a $3d^8$ configuration. Cl$^-$ is a weak field ligand. The complex is tetrahedral with $2$ unpaired electrons. (Paramagnetic)

3. $[\text{Ni(CN)}_4]^{2-}$: Ni$^{2+}$ has a $3d^8$ configuration. CN$^-$ is a strong field ligand. The complex is square planar ($dsp^2$) with all electrons paired. (Diamagnetic)

4. $[\text{Ni(CO)}_4]$: Ni$^0$ has a $3d^8 4s^2$ configuration, which becomes $3d^{10}$ in the presence of the strong field ligand CO. The complex is tetrahedral with all electrons paired. (Diamagnetic)

5. $[\text{CoF}_6]^{3-}$: Co$^{3+}$ has a $3d^6$ configuration. F$^-$ is a weak field ligand. The complex is octahedral and high spin ($t_{2g}^4 e_g^2$) with $4$ unpaired electrons. (Paramagnetic)

6. $[\text{Fe(CN)}_6]^{4-}$: Fe$^{2+}$ has a $3d^6$ configuration. CN$^-$ is a strong field ligand. The complex is octahedral and low spin ($t_{2g}^6 e_g^0$) with all electrons paired. (Diamagnetic)

7. $[\text{Mn(CN)}_6]^{3-}$: Mn$^{3+}$ has a $3d^4$ configuration. CN$^-$ is a strong field ligand. The complex is octahedral and low spin ($t_{2g}^4 e_g^0$) with $2$ unpaired electrons. (Paramagnetic)

8. $[\text{Ti(CN)}_6]^{3-}$: Ti$^{3+}$ has a $3d^1$ configuration. The complex is octahedral ($t_{2g}^1 e_g^0$) with $1$ unpaired electron. (Paramagnetic)

9. $[\text{Cu(H}_2\text{O)}_6]^{2+}$: Cu$^{2+}$ has a $3d^9$ configuration. The complex is octahedral ($t_{2g}^6 e_g^3$) with $1$ unpaired electron. (Paramagnetic)

10. $[\text{Co(C}_2\text{O}_4\text{)}_3]^{3-}$: Co$^{3+}$ has a $3d^6$ configuration. Oxalate ($\text{C}_2\text{O}_4^{2-}$) acts as a strong field ligand for Co$^{3+}$. The complex is octahedral and low spin ($t_{2g}^6 e_g^0$) with all electrons paired. (Diamagnetic)

Therefore, the paramagnetic complexes are $[\text{MnBr}_4]^{2-}$, $[\text{NiCl}_4]^{2-}$, $[\text{CoF}_6]^{3-}$, $[\text{Mn(CN)}_6]^{3-}$, $[\text{Ti(CN)}_6]^{3-}$, and $[\text{Cu(H}_2\text{O)}_6]^{2+}$.

The total number of paramagnetic complexes is $6$.

Answer: $6$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following sequences of hybridisation, geometry and magnetic nature are correct for the given coordination compounds ?
A. $[\text{NiCl}_4]^{2-}$ $-$ sp$^3$, tetrahedral, paramagnetic
B. $[\text{Ni(NH}_3)_6]^{2+}$ $-$ sp$^3$d$^2$, octahedral, paramagnetic
C. $[\text{Ni(CO)}_4]$ $-$ sp$^3$, tetrahedral, paramagnetic
D. $[\text{Ni(CN)}_4]^{2-}$ $-$ dsp$^2$, square planar, diamagnetic
Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, B, C and D
- **(B)** B, C and D only
- **(C)** A, C and D only
- **(D)** A, B and D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A, B and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

In $[\text{NiCl}_4]^{2-}$, Ni is in $+2$ oxidation state with a $3d^8$ configuration. Since $\text{Cl}^-$ is a weak field ligand, no pairing of electrons takes place. The hybridisation is $sp^3$, the geometry is tetrahedral, and the presence of two unpaired electrons makes it paramagnetic. Thus, statement A is correct.

In $[\text{Ni(NH}_3)_6]^{2+}$, Ni is in $+2$ oxidation state with a $3d^8$ configuration. In an octahedral field, a $d^8$ ion cannot form inner orbital complexes. It undergoes $sp^3d^2$ hybridisation, forming an octahedral geometry. It has two unpaired electrons, making it paramagnetic. Thus, statement B is correct.

In $[\text{Ni(CO)}_4]$, Ni is in $0$ oxidation state with a $3d^8 4s^2$ configuration. Since $\text{CO}$ is a strong field ligand, the $4s$ electrons are forced to pair up in the $3d$ orbitals, resulting in a $3d^{10}$ configuration. The hybridisation is $sp^3$, the geometry is tetrahedral, and since there are no unpaired electrons, it is diamagnetic. Statement C incorrectly lists it as paramagnetic.

In $[\text{Ni(CN)}_4]^{2-}$, Ni is in $+2$ oxidation state with a $3d^8$ configuration. Since $\text{CN}^-$ is a strong field ligand, it causes the pairing of $3d$ electrons, leaving one $3d$ orbital empty. The hybridisation is $dsp^2$, the geometry is square planar, and the absence of unpaired electrons makes it diamagnetic. Thus, statement D is correct.

Therefore, only statements A, B, and D are correct.

Answer: A, B and D only

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the metal complexes $[Ni(en)_3]^{2+}$ (A), $[NiCl_4]^{2-}$ (B) and $[Ni(NH_3)_6]^{2+}$ (C). Choose the CORRECT option by considering the number of unpaired electrons present in (A), (B) and (C) respectively and the order of frequency of absorption.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2, 2, 2$ and $(A)>(C)>(B)$
- **(B)** $0, 2, 0$ and $(A)>(C)>(B)$
- **(C)** $2, 2, 0$ and $(B)>(C)>(A)$
- **(D)** $2, 2, 2$ and $(C)>(A)>(B)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $2, 2, 2$ and $(A)>(C)>(B)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The oxidation state of $Ni$ in all three complexes is $+2$. The electronic configuration of $Ni^{2+}$ is $[Ar] 3d^{8}$.

In $[Ni(en)_3]^{2+}$ (A), the coordination number is $6$ (octahedral geometry). The $d^{8}$ configuration in an octahedral field is $t_{2g}^{6} e_{g}^{2}$, which has $2$ unpaired electrons.

In $[NiCl_4]^{2-}$ (B), the coordination number is $4$ with weak field $Cl^{-}$ ligands (tetrahedral geometry). The $d^{8}$ configuration in a tetrahedral field is $e^{4} t_{2}^{4}$, which has $2$ unpaired electrons.

In $[Ni(NH_3)_6]^{2+}$ (C), the coordination number is $6$ (octahedral geometry). The $d^{8}$ configuration in an octahedral field is $t_{2g}^{6} e_{g}^{2}$, which has $2$ unpaired electrons.

Thus, the number of unpaired electrons in (A), (B), and (C) are $2, 2,$ and $2$ respectively.

The frequency of absorption of light is directly proportional to the crystal field splitting energy ($\Delta$).

According to the spectrochemical series, the field strength of the ligands follows the order: $en > NH_3 > Cl^{-}$.

Furthermore, the crystal field splitting energy for octahedral complexes is significantly greater than that for tetrahedral complexes ($\Delta_o = \dfrac{9}{4}\Delta_t$).

Therefore, the order of crystal field splitting energy, and hence the frequency of absorption, is $[Ni(en)_3]^{2+} > [Ni(NH_3)_6]^{2+} > [NiCl_4]^{2-}$, which corresponds to $(A) > (C) > (B)$.

Answer: $2, 2, 2$ and $(A)>(C)>(B)$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Match the LIST-I with LIST-II
<table style="border: 1px solid black; border-collapse: collapse; width: auto;"><tr><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List-I
Complex ion</th><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List-II
Calculated spin only magnetic moment (BM)</th></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">A. $[Cr(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">I. $3.87$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">B. $[Co(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">II. $5.92$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">C. $[Cu(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">III. $4.90$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">D. $[Mn(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">IV. $1.73$</td></tr></table>
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-I, B-III, C-IV, D-II
- **(B)** A-II, B-I, C-III, D-IV
- **(C)** A-IV, B-II, C-I, D-III
- **(D)** A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II**

**Detailed Solution:**

The spin-only magnetic moment is given by the formula $\mu = \sqrt{n(n+2)}$ BM, where $n$ is the number of unpaired electrons.

A. $[Cr(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$: Chromium is in the $+2$ oxidation state. The electronic configuration of $Cr^{2+}$ is $[Ar] 3d^4$. Since $H_2O$ is a weak field ligand, no pairing occurs. The number of unpaired electrons is $n = 4$.
$\mu = \sqrt{4(4+2)} = \sqrt{24} \approx 4.90$ BM. (A $\rightarrow$ III)

B. $[Co(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$: Cobalt is in the $+2$ oxidation state. The electronic configuration of $Co^{2+}$ is $[Ar] 3d^7$. With $H_2O$ as a weak field ligand, the configuration is $t_{2g}^5 e_g^2$. The number of unpaired electrons is $n = 3$.
$\mu = \sqrt{3(3+2)} = \sqrt{15} \approx 3.87$ BM. (B $\rightarrow$ I)

C. $[Cu(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$: Copper is in the $+2$ oxidation state. The electronic configuration of $Cu^{2+}$ is $[Ar] 3d^9$. The number of unpaired electrons is $n = 1$.
$\mu = \sqrt{1(1+2)} = \sqrt{3} \approx 1.73$ BM. (C $\rightarrow$ IV)

D. $[Mn(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$: Manganese is in the $+2$ oxidation state. The electronic configuration of $Mn^{2+}$ is $[Ar] 3d^5$. With $H_2O$ as a weak field ligand, the configuration is $t_{2g}^3 e_g^2$. The number of unpaired electrons is $n = 5$.
$\mu = \sqrt{5(5+2)} = \sqrt{35} \approx 5.92$ BM. (D $\rightarrow$ II)

The correct matching is A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II.

Answer: A-III, B-I, C-IV, D-II

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$5.33$ g of $CrCl_3 \cdot 6H_2O$, which is a $1 : 3$ electrolyte, is dissolved in water and is passed through a cation exchanger. The chloride ions in the eluted solution, on treatment with $AgNO_3$ results in $8.61$ g of $AgCl$. The ratio of moles of complex reacted and moles of $AgCl$ formed is _______ $\times 10^{-2}$. (Nearest integer)
[Molar mass in g mol$^{-1}$ Cr : $52$, Ag : $108$, Cl : $35.5$, H : $1$, O : $16$]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **33**

**Detailed Solution:**

Molar mass of $CrCl_3 \cdot 6H_2O = 52 + 3 \times 35.5 + 6 \times 18 = 266.5 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Moles of complex reacted $= \dfrac{5.33}{266.5} = 0.02 \text{ mol}$

Since the complex is a $1:3$ electrolyte, it dissociates to give one complex cation and three chloride anions. Its formula is $[Cr(H_2O)_6]Cl_3$.

When passed through a cation exchanger, the complex cation is exchanged, and the chloride ions pass into the eluted solution.

Molar mass of $AgCl = 108 + 35.5 = 143.5 \text{ g mol}^{-1}$

Moles of $AgCl$ formed $= \dfrac{8.61}{143.5} = 0.06 \text{ mol}$

The ratio of moles of complex reacted to moles of $AgCl$ formed is:

$\text{Ratio} = \dfrac{0.02}{0.06} = \dfrac{1}{3} = 0.3333...$

This can be written as $33.33 \times 10^{-2}$.

Rounding to the nearest integer, we get $33$.

Answer: $33$

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The correct increasing order of spin-only magnetic moment values of the complex ions $\left[\mathrm{MnBr}_{4}\right]^{2-}(\mathrm{A}),\left[\mathrm{Cu}\left(\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}\right)_{6}\right]^{2+}(\mathrm{B}),\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}(\mathrm{C})$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ni}\left(\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}\right)_{6}\right]^{2+}(\mathrm{D})$ is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{D} < \mathrm{B} < \mathrm{A}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{C} < \mathrm{B} < \mathrm{D} < \mathrm{A}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{A}=\mathrm{B} < \mathrm{D} < \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $A=B < C < D$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{C} < \mathrm{B} < \mathrm{D} < \mathrm{A}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Calculate spin-only magnetic moment using $\mu_s = \sqrt{n(n+2)}$ where $n$ is the number of unpaired electrons.
For $[\text{MnBr}_4]^{2-}$ (A): Mn(II) in tetrahedral environment is high spin with 5 unpaired electrons. $\mu_s = \sqrt{5(7)} = \sqrt{35} \approx 5.92$ BM.
For $[\text{Cu}(\text{H}_2\text{O})_6]^{2+}$ (B): Cu(II) has 1 unpaired electron. $\mu_s = \sqrt{1(3)} = \sqrt{3} \approx 1.73$ BM.
For $[\text{Ni}(\text{CN})_4]^{2-}$ (C): Ni(II) in square planar geometry is low spin with 0 unpaired electrons. $\mu_s = 0$ BM.
For $[\text{Ni}(\text{H}_2\text{O})_6]^{2+}$ (D): Ni(II) in octahedral environment is high spin with 2 unpaired electrons. $\mu_s = \sqrt{2(4)} = \sqrt{8} \approx 2.83$ BM.
Increasing order: C (0) < B (1.73) < D (2.83) < A (5.92)

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct statement among the following is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_{4}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}$ are diamagnetic and $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_{4}\right]^{2-}$ is paramagnetic.
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_{4}$ and $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_{4}\right]^{2-}$ are diamagnetic and $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}$ is paramagnetic.
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}$ and $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_{4}\right]^{2-}$ are diamagnetic and $\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_{4}$ is paramagnetic.
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_{4}$ is diamagnetic and $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_{4}\right]^{2-}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}$ are paramagnetic.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_{4}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}$ are diamagnetic and $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_{4}\right]^{2-}$ is paramagnetic.**

**Detailed Solution:**

In $Ni(CO)_4$, Nickel is in $0$ oxidation state with configuration $[Ar]3d^8 4s^2$. $CO$ is a strong field ligand, causing pairing of $4s$ electrons into $3d$ orbitals, resulting in $3d^{10}$ configuration. It is $sp^3$ hybridized and diamagnetic (zero unpaired electrons).
In $[Ni(CN)_4]^{2-}$, Nickel is in $+2$ oxidation state with configuration $[Ar]3d^8$. $CN^-$ is a strong field ligand, causing pairing of the two unpaired electrons in $3d$ orbitals. It is $dsp^2$ hybridized (square planar) and diamagnetic.
In $[NiCl_4]^{2-}$, Nickel is in $+2$ oxidation state with configuration $[Ar]3d^8$. $Cl^-$ is a weak field ligand and cannot cause pairing of electrons. It has two unpaired electrons in $3d$ orbitals, making it $sp^3$ hybridized (tetrahedral) and paramagnetic.
Therefore, $Ni(CO)_4$ and $[Ni(CN)_4]^{2-}$ are diamagnetic, while $[NiCl_4]^{2-}$ is paramagnetic.

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A chromium complex with a formula $\mathrm{CrCl}_{3} \cdot 6 \mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}$ has a spin only magnetic moment value of 3.87 BM and its solution conductivity corresponds to $1: 2$ electrolyte. 2.75 g of the complex solution was initially passed through a cation exchanger. The solution obtained after the process was reacted with excess of $\mathrm{AgNO}_{3}$. The amount of AgCl formed in the above process is $\_\_\_\_$ g. (Nearest integer) 
 [Given: Molar mass in $\mathrm{g} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{Cr}: 52 ; \mathrm{Cl}: 35.5, \mathrm{Ag}: 108, \mathrm{O}: 16, \mathrm{H}: 1$]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

The complex $\text{CrCl}_3 \cdot 6\text{H}_2\text{O}$ with magnetic moment 3.87 BM indicates $3.87 = \sqrt{n(n+2)}$ giving n = 3 unpaired electrons, confirming Cr³⁺.
A 1:2 electrolyte means structure is $[\text{Cr(H}_2\text{O})_5\text{Cl}]^{2+}\text{Cl}_2^-$.
Molar mass = 52 + 5(18) + 35.5 + 6(18) = 285.5 g/mol.
Moles of complex = 2.75/285.5 = 0.00963 mol.
After cation exchange, 2 Cl⁻ remain per formula unit (from the 1:2 electrolyte), giving 0.00963 × 2 = 0.01926 mol of free Cl⁻.
Reacting with excess AgNO₃: mass of AgCl = 0.01926 × 143.5 = 2.76 g ≈ 3 g (nearest integer).

</details>

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given below are two statements: 
 <b>Statement I</b>: The number of paramagnetic species among $\left[\mathrm{CoF}_{6}\right]^{3-},\left[\mathrm{TiF}_{6}\right]^{3-}$, $\mathrm{V}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{5}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_{6}\right]^{3-}$ is 3. 
 <b>Statement II</b>: $\mathrm{K}_{4}\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_{6}\right]<\mathrm{K}_{3}\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_{6}\right]<\left[\mathrm{Fe}\left(\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}\right)_{6}\right] \mathrm{SO}_{4} \cdot \mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}<\left[\mathrm{Fe}\left(\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}\right)_{6}\right] \mathrm{Cl}_{3}$ is the correct order in terms of number of unpaired electron(s) present in the complexes. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I: Analysis of paramagnetic species.
1. $[CoF_6]^{3-}$: $Co^{3+}$ is $3d^6$. $F^-$ is a weak field ligand (WFL), so configuration is $t_{2g}^4 e_g^2$. Unpaired electrons ($n$) = 4. Paramagnetic.
2. $[TiF_6]^{3-}$: $Ti^{3+}$ is $3d^1$. $n = 1$. Paramagnetic.
3. $V_2O_5$: $V^{5+}$ is $3d^0$. $n = 0$. Diamagnetic.
4. $[Fe(CN)_6]^{3-}$: $Fe^{3+}$ is $3d^5$. $CN^-$ is a strong field ligand (SFL), so configuration is $t_{2g}^5 e_g^0$. $n = 1$. Paramagnetic.
Total paramagnetic species = 3. Statement I is true.
Statement II: Analysis of unpaired electrons.
1. $K_4[Fe(CN)_6]$: $Fe^{2+}$ ($3d^6$) with SFL $\rightarrow t_{2g}^6 e_g^0$, $n = 0$.
2. $K_3[Fe(CN)_6]$: $Fe^{3+}$ ($3d^5$) with SFL $\rightarrow t_{2g}^5 e_g^0$, $n = 1$.
3. $[Fe(H_2O)_6]SO_4 \cdot H_2O$: $Fe^{2+}$ ($3d^6$) with WFL $\rightarrow t_{2g}^4 e_g^2$, $n = 4$.
4. $[Fe(H_2O)_6]Cl_3$: $Fe^{3+}$ ($3d^5$) with WFL $\rightarrow t_{2g}^3 e_g^2$, $n = 5$.
Order of $n$: $0 < 1 < 4 < 5$. Statement II is true.
Both statements are true.

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given below are two statements: 
 <b>Statement I</b>: Hybridisation, shape and spin only magnetic moment of $\mathrm{K}_{3}\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{CO}_{3}\right)_{3}\right]$ is $\mathrm{sp}^{3} \mathrm{~d}^{2}$, octahedral and 4.9 BM respectively. 
 <b>Statement II</b>: Geometry, hybridisation and spin only magnetic moment values $(\mathrm{BM})$ of the ions $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-},\left[\mathrm{MnBr}_{4}\right]^{2-}$ and $\left[\mathrm{CoF}_{6}\right]^{3-}$ respectively are square planar, tetrahedral, octahedral; $\mathrm{dsp}^{2}, \mathrm{sp}^{3}, \mathrm{sp}^{3} \mathrm{~d}^{2}$ and $0,5.9,4.9$. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

In Statement I, for $K_3[Co(CO_3)_3]$, Cobalt is in $+3$ oxidation state ($Co^{3+}: 3d^6$). Carbonate ($CO_3^{2-}$) is a weak field ligand. Thus, no pairing occurs, resulting in 4 unpaired electrons. Hybridisation is $sp^3d^2$, shape is octahedral, and $\mu = \sqrt{4(4+2)} = \sqrt{24} \approx 4.9$ BM. Statement I is true.
In Statement II:
For $[Ni(CN)_4]^{2-}$, $Ni^{2+}$ is $3d^8$. $CN^-$ is a strong field ligand, causing pairing. Hybridisation is $dsp^2$, geometry is square planar, and $\mu = 0$ BM.
For $[MnBr_4]^{2-}$, $Mn^{2+}$ is $3d^5$. $Br^-$ is a weak field ligand. Hybridisation is $sp^3$, geometry is tetrahedral, and $\mu = \sqrt{5(5+2)} = \sqrt{35} \approx 5.9$ BM.
For $[CoF_6]^{3-}$, $Co^{3+}$ is $3d^6$. $F^-$ is a weak field ligand. Hybridisation is $sp^3d^2$, geometry is octahedral, and $\mu = \sqrt{4(4+2)} = \sqrt{24} \approx 4.9$ BM.
Statement II is true.
Both statements are true.

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Total number of unpaired electrons present in the central metal atoms/ions of $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_{4}\right],\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_{4}\right]^{2-},\left[\mathrm{PtCl}_{2}\left(\mathrm{NH}_{3}\right)_{2}\right],\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Pt}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}$ is $\_\_\_\_$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

$[Ni(CO)_4]$:
Ni is zero-valent with configuration $[Ar]3d^{10}4s^2$.
CO is a strong field ligand causing complete pairing in the filled $3d^{10}$ orbital.
Unpaired electrons = 0.

$[NiCl_4]^{2-}$:
Ni is $+2$ with configuration $[Ar]3d^8$.
Cl is a weak field ligand producing high-spin tetrahedral geometry with configuration $3d^8$: five orbitals with two electrons paired, three electrons unpaired.
Unpaired electrons = 2.

$[PtCl_2(NH_3)_2]$:
Pt is $+2$ with configuration $[Xe]4f^{14}5d^8$.
Pt(II) adopts square planar geometry with strong-field splitting leading to complete pairing: $5d^8$ configuration places all electrons in bonding orbitals.
Unpaired electrons = 0.

$[Ni(CN)_4]^{2-}$:
Ni is $+2$ with $3d^8$ configuration.
CN is a strong field ligand.
Square planar geometry is preferred, resulting in all electrons paired.
Unpaired electrons = 0.

$[Pt(CN)_4]^{2-}$:
Pt is $+2$ with $5d^8$ configuration.
CN is a strong field ligand producing square planar geometry with complete pairing.
Unpaired electrons = 0.

Total unpaired electrons = 0 + 2 + 0 + 0 + 0 = 2.

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Identify the CORRECT set of details from the following: 
 A. $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_{3}\right)_{6}\right]^{3+}$ : Inner orbital complex; $\mathrm{d}^{2} \mathrm{sp}^{3}$ hybridized 
 B. $\left[\mathrm{MnCl}_{6}\right]^{3-}$ : Outer orbital complex; $\mathrm{sp}^{3} \mathrm{~d}^{2}$ hybridized 
 C. $\left[\mathrm{CoF}_{6}\right]^{3-}$ : Outer orbital complex; $\mathrm{d}^{2} \mathrm{sp}^{3}$ hybridized 
 D. $\left[\mathrm{FeF}_{6}\right]^{3-}$ : Outer orbital complex; $\mathrm{sp}^{3} \mathrm{~d}^{2}$ hybridized 
 E. $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}$ : Inner orbital complex; $\mathrm{sp}^{3}$ hybridized 
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, C & E Only
- **(B)** A, B & D Only
- **(C)** C & D Only
- **(D)** A, B, C, D & E

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A, B & D Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Analyzing each complex based on Valence Bond Theory (VBT):
A. $[Co(NH_3)_6]^{3+}$: 
$Co^{3+}$ is $3d^6$. $NH_3$ is a strong field ligand (SFL) for $Co^{3+}$, causing pairing. This leaves two $3d$ orbitals vacant. Hybridization is $d^2sp^3$ (Inner orbital complex). Statement A is correct.

B. $[MnCl_6]^{3-}$: 
$Mn^{3+}$ is $3d^4$. $Cl^-$ is a weak field ligand (WFL). No pairing occurs. To accommodate 6 ligands, it uses $4s$, $4p$, and $4d$ orbitals. Hybridization is $sp^3d^2$ (Outer orbital complex). Statement B is correct.

C. $[CoF_6]^{3-}$: 
$Co^{3+}$ is $3d^6$. $F^-$ is a WFL. No pairing occurs. Hybridization is $sp^3d^2$ (Outer orbital complex). Statement C is incorrect because it mentions $d^2sp^3$.

D. $[FeF_6]^{3-}$: 
$Fe^{3+}$ is $3d^5$. $F^-$ is a WFL. No pairing occurs. Hybridization is $sp^3d^2$ (Outer orbital complex). Statement D is correct.

E. $[Ni(CN)_4]^{2-}$: 
$Ni^{2+}$ is $3d^8$. $CN^-$ is a SFL, causing pairing of the two unpaired electrons in $3d$. This leaves one $3d$ orbital vacant. Hybridization is $dsp^2$ (Square planar, Inner orbital complex). Statement E is incorrect because it mentions $sp^3$.

Correct statements are A, B, and D.

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The statements that are incorrect about the nickel(II) complex of dimethylglyoxime are : 
 A. It is red in colour. 
 B. It has a high solubility in water at $\mathrm{pH}=9$. 
 C. The Ni ion has two unpaired d-electrons. 
 D. The $\mathrm{N}-\mathrm{Ni}-\mathrm{N}$ bond angle is almost close to $90^{\circ}$. 
 E. The complex contains four five-membered metallacycles (metal containing rings). 
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** C and D Only
- **(B)** C and E Only
- **(C)** B, C and E Only
- **(D)** A, D and B Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) B, C and E Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement A is incorrect as the complex is yellow-green, not red.
Statement B is incorrect because the complex shows low solubility in water, especially at neutral pH.
Statement C is incorrect because Ni(II) in square planar geometry has all d-electrons paired (d^8 configuration), not unpaired electrons.
Statement D is correct regarding the N-Ni-N bond angle being approximately 90° in square planar geometry.
Statement E is correct as the complex contains two five-membered metallacycles (chelate rings) per dimethylglyoxime molecule.
The incorrect statements are B, C, and E.

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given below are two statements : 
 <b>Statement I</b> : Among $\left.\left[\mathrm{Cu}\left(\mathrm{NH}_{3}\right)_{4}\right]^{2+},\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{en})_{3}\right)\right]^{2+},\left[\mathrm{Ni}\left(\mathrm{NH}_{3}\right)_{6}\right]^{2+}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Mn}\left(\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}\right)_{6}\right]^{2+}$, $\left[\mathrm{Mn}\left(\mathrm{H}_{2} \mathrm{O}\right)_{6}\right]^{2+}$ has the maximum number of unpaired electrons. 
 <b>Statement II</b> : The number of pairs among $\left\{\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_{4}\right]^{2-},\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_{4}\right]\right\},\left\{\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_{4}\right]^{2-},\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}\right\}$ and $\left\{\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_{4}\right],\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_{4}\right]^{2-}\right\}$ that contain only diamagnetic species is two. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below : Statement I is true but Statement II is false Both Statement I and Statement II are false

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Statement I is true but Statement II is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I: Unpaired electrons in each complex:
$[Cu(NH_3)_4]^{2+}$: $Cu^{2+}$ is $d^9$ → 1 unpaired
$[Ni(en)_3]^{2+}$: $Ni^{2+}$ is $d^8$, octahedral → 2 unpaired
$[Ni(NH_3)_6]^{2+}$: $Ni^{2+}$ is $d^8$ → 2 unpaired
$[Mn(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$: $Mn^{2+}$ is $d^5$, weak field → high spin → 5 unpaired
$[Mn(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$ has maximum. Statement I is true.
Statement II: $[NiCl_4]^{2-}$: $Ni^{2+}$ ($d^8$), tetrahedral → paramagnetic
$[Ni(CO)_4]$: $Ni^0$ ($d^{10}$) → diamagnetic
$[Ni(CN)_4]^{2-}$: $Ni^{2+}$ ($d^8$), square planar → diamagnetic
Only pair $\{[Ni(CO)_4], [Ni(CN)_4]^{2-}\}$ has both diamagnetic. Count = 1, not 2. Statement II is false.

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2025 (8 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/FVeV8DNF3J2mwBMr0OuVb0z0KN_LoLKvjMjpwNz0Y7E.original.fullsize.png"/>

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5$
- **(B)** $6$
- **(C)** $4$
- **(D)** $3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right]_6\right]^{3+}, \mathrm{Co}^{+3}:[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^6, \mathrm{NH}_3$ is S.F.L
Hybridisation state of $\mathrm{Co}^{3+}$ is $\mathrm{d}^2 \mathrm{sp}^3$
In $\mathrm{SF}_6$, Hybridisation state of sulphur is $\mathrm{sp}^3 \mathrm{~d}^2$
In $\left[\mathrm{CrF}_6\right]^{3-}, \mathrm{Cr}^{+3}:[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^3$
Hybridisation state of $\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}$ is $\mathrm{d}^2 \mathrm{sp}^3$
$\left[\mathrm{CoF}_6\right]^{3-}, \mathrm{Co}^{+3}:[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^6 \mathrm{~F}^{-}$is W.F.L
Hybridisation state of $\mathrm{Co}^{3+}$ is $\mathrm{sp}^3 \mathrm{~d}^2$
$\left[\mathrm{Mn}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}, \mathrm{Mn}^{+3}:[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^4 \mathrm{CN}^{-}$is S.F.L Hybridisation state of $\mathrm{Mn}^{3+}$ is $\mathrm{d}^2 \mathrm{sp}^3$
$\left[\mathrm{MnCl}_6\right]^{3-}, \mathrm{Mn}^{+3}:[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^4 \mathrm{Cl}^{-}$is W.F.L
Hybridisation state of $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$is $\mathrm{sp}^3 \mathrm{~d}^2$
Total number of $\mathrm{sp}^3 \mathrm{~d}^2$ hybridized molecules is 3

</details>

### Q20. JEE Main 2025 (4 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which one of the following complexes will have $\Delta_0=0$ and $\mu=5.96$ B.M.?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^4$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{CO}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{FeF}_6\right]^4$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{Mn}(\mathrm{SCN})_6\right]^4$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\left[\mathrm{Mn}(\mathrm{SCN})_6\right]^4$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(1) $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+} \quad \mathrm{Co}^{3+} \Rightarrow 3 \mathrm{~d}^6 4 \mathrm{~s}^0$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/tlN7QE7ms1XLmmW2k1bSQkXZ_O7a96mAYpsBEz2KOtM.original.fullsize.png"/>
$=[-0.4 \times 6+0.6 \times(0)] \Delta_0=-2.4 \Delta_0$
(2)
$\begin{aligned}
& {\left[\mathrm{Mn}(\mathrm{SCN})_6\right]^4} \\
& \mathrm{Mn}^{2+} \Rightarrow 3 \mathrm{~d}^5 4 \mathrm{~s}^0
\end{aligned}$<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/96xXKp8GLWwc3l-gyaY4nyDhlZ43mT9q4bydGc9i1lA.original.fullsize.png"/>
$\begin{aligned}
& \mu=\sqrt{35} \text { B.M. }=5.96 \text { B.M. } \\
& \mathrm{CFSE}=(-0.4 \times 3+0.6 \times 2) \Delta_0
\end{aligned}$
So $\Delta_0=0$
(3)
$\begin{aligned}
& {\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{-4} \quad \mathrm{Fe}^{2+} \Rightarrow 3 \mathrm{~d}^6 4 \mathrm{~s}^0}
\end{aligned}$<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/mXbqJQQCxwBreyBTg7CuNsvWFhP334psJSplxk8tsy4.original.fullsize.png"/>
$\mathrm{CFSE}=-2.4 \Delta_0$
(4) $\left[\mathrm{FeF}_6\right]^{4-}$
$\mathrm{Fe}^{2+} \Rightarrow 3 \mathrm{~d}^6 4 \mathrm{~s}^0$<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/zqvcznomxHPLkpqdaC4cOWvkldF0f22bBKDvUFo9-cY.original.fullsize.png"/>
$\begin{aligned}
& \mu=\sqrt{24} \text { B.M. }=4.89 \text { B.M. } \\
& \text { CFSE }=(-0.4 \times 4+0.6 \times 2) \Delta_0=-1.2 \Delta_0
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The spin-only magnetic moment value of $\mathrm{M}^{\mathrm{n+-}}$ ion formed among $\mathrm{Ni}, \mathrm{Zn} \mathrm{Mn}$ and Cu that has the least enthalpy of atomisation is ________. (in nearest integer)
Here n is equal to the number of diamagnetic complexes among $\mathrm{K}_2\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right],\left[\mathrm{Zn}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right] \mathrm{Cl}_2$,
$\mathrm{K}_3\left[\mathrm{Mn}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right] \text { and }\left[\mathrm{Cu}\left(\mathrm{PPh}_3\right)_3 \mathrm{I}\right]$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

$\left[\mathrm{Zn}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right] \mathrm{Cl}_2 \Rightarrow \mathrm{sp}^3 \mathrm{~d}^2$, Diamagnetic
$\mathrm{K}_3\left[\mathrm{Mn}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right] \Rightarrow \mathrm{d}^2 \mathrm{sp}^3$, Paramagnetic
$\left[\mathrm{Cu}\left(\mathrm{PPh}_3\right)_3 \mathrm{I}\right] \Rightarrow \mathrm{sp}^3$, Diamagnetic
Hence the value of $n$ is 2
Least value of enthalpy of atomisation among Ni ,
$\mathrm{Zn}, \mathrm{Mn}$ and Cu is of Zn
$\begin{aligned}

& \mu=0
\end{aligned}$

</details>

- **(B)** (A) $-(\mathrm{III}),(\mathrm{B})-(\mathrm{I}),(\mathrm{C})-(\mathrm{II}),(\mathrm{D})-(\mathrm{IV})$

### Q28. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

One mole of the octahedral complex compound $\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}_3$ gives 3 moles of ions on dissolution in water. One mole of the same complex reacts with excess of $\mathrm{AgNO}_3$ solution to yield two moles of $\mathrm{AgCl}_{(\mathrm{s})}$. The structure of the complex is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4 \mathrm{Cl}_2\right] \cdot \mathrm{Cl} \cdot \mathrm{NH}_3$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_3 \mathrm{Cl}_3\right] \cdot 2 \mathrm{NH}_3$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}\right] \mathrm{Cl}_2$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4 \mathrm{Cl}\right] \cdot \mathrm{Cl}_2 \cdot \mathrm{NH}_3$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}\right] \mathrm{Cl}_2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{array}{l}{\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}\right] \mathrm{Cl}_2 \rightarrow \underbrace{\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}\right]^{2+}(\text { aq. })+2 \mathrm{Cl}^{-}(\text {aq. })}_{3 \text { ions in water }}} \\ {\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}\right] \mathrm{Cl}_2(\text { aq. })+2 \mathrm{AgNO}_3(\text { aq. }) \rightarrow} {\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}\right]\left(\mathrm{NO}_3\right)_2(\text { aq. })+2 \mathrm{AgCl}(\mathrm{s})}\end{array}$

</details>

### Q29. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

$\mathrm{CrCl}_3 \cdot \mathrm{xNH}_3$ can exist as a complex. 0.1 molal aqueous solution of this complex shows a depression in freezing point of $0.558^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$. Assuming $100 \%$ ionisation of this complex and coordination number of Cr is 6 , the complex will be
(Given $\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{f}}=1.86 \mathrm{~K} \mathrm{~kg} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}\right] \mathrm{Cl}_2$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right] \mathrm{Cl}_3$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_3 \mathrm{Cl}_3\right]$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4 \mathrm{Cl}_2\right] \mathrm{Cl}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}\right] \mathrm{Cl}_2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta T_f=i K_f m \\ & 0.558=i \times 1.86 \times 0.1 \\ & i=\frac{0.558}{0.186}=3\end{aligned}$
Number of ions when 100\% ionisation takes place $=3$
$\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}\right] \mathrm{Cl}_2 \xrightarrow{\text { aqueous solution }}\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_5 \mathrm{Cl}^{2+}\right]^{2+}+2 \mathrm{Cl}^{-}$
Number of ions $=3$

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Identify the homoleptic complex(es) that is/are low spin.
(A) $\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_5 \mathrm{NO}\right]^{2-}$
(B) $\left[\mathrm{CoF}_6\right]^{3-}$
(C) $\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{4-}$
(D) $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+}$
(E) $\quad\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}$

Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** (C) only
- **(B)** (C) and (D) only
- **(C)** (A) and (C) only
- **(D)** (B) and (E) only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (C) and (D) only**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A) $\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_5 \mathrm{NO}\right]^{-2} \rightarrow$ Heteroleptic, $\mathrm{Fe}^{+2}, 3 \mathrm{~d}^6$, $\mathrm{t}_{2 \mathrm{~g}}{ }^6 \mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{g}}{ }^0, \mathrm{~d}^2 \mathrm{sp}^3$, Low spin ( 3 d series +SFL )
(B) $\left[\mathrm{CoF}_6\right]^{-3} \rightarrow$ Homoleptic, $\mathrm{sp}^3 \mathrm{~d}^2$, High spin, $\mathrm{Co}^{+3}, 3 \mathrm{~d}^6(3 \mathrm{~d}$ series + WFL $)$
(C) $\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{-4} \rightarrow$ Homoleptic
$\mathrm{Fe}^{+2}, 3 \mathrm{~d}^6, \mathrm{~d}^2 \mathrm{sp}^3, \mathrm{t}_{2 \mathrm{~g}}{ }^6 \mathrm{eg}^0$ Low spin
$(3 \mathrm{~d}$ series +SFL$)$
(D) $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{+3} \rightarrow$ Homoleptic, $\mathrm{Co}^{+3} 3 \mathrm{~d}^6, \mathrm{~d}^2 \mathrm{sp}^3$,
$\mathrm{t}_{2 \mathrm{~g}}{ }^6 \mathrm{eg}^0$, Low spin (3d series + SFL)
(E) $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{+2} \rightarrow$ Homoleptic
$\mathrm{Cr}^{+2} 3 \mathrm{~d}^4, \mathrm{~d}^2 \mathrm{sp}^3$, High spin $\mathrm{t}_{2 \mathrm{~g}}{ }^3 \mathrm{e}_{\mathrm{g}}{ }^1$
(3d series + WFL)

</details>

### Q31. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

From the magnetic behaviour of $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}$ (paramagnetic) and $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]$ (diamagnetic), choose the correct geometry and oxidation state.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}: \mathrm{Ni}^{\mathrm{II}}$, tetrahedral
$\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]: \mathrm{Ni}^{\mathrm{II}}$, square planar
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}$ : $\mathrm{Ni}^{\mathrm{II}}$, square planar
$\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]: \mathrm{Ni}(0)$, square planar
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}: \mathrm{Ni}^{\mathrm{II}}$, tetrahedral
$\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]: \mathrm{Ni}(0)$, tetrahedral
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}: \mathrm{Ni}(0)$, tetrahedral
$\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]: \mathrm{Ni}(0)$, square planar

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}: \mathrm{Ni}^{\mathrm{II}}$, tetrahedral
$\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]: \mathrm{Ni}(0)$, tetrahedral**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}$
$\mathrm{Ni}^{+2}-[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^8 4 \mathrm{~s}^0 \rightarrow \mathrm{sp}^3$, Tetrahedral
Number of unpaired electron $=2$ paramagentic
$\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]$
$\mathrm{Ni}(0) \rightarrow[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^{10} 4 \mathrm{~s}^0$ (After rearrangement)
No unpaired electron
$\mathrm{sp}^3$, Tetrahedral, Diamagnetic

</details>

### Q36. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Match List - I with List - II.
\(\begin{array}{lll} 
\begin{array}{l}
\text { List - I } \\
\text { (Complex ion) }
\end{array} & \begin{array}{l}
\text { List - II } \\
\text { (Spin only magnetic moment in B.M.) }
\end{array} \\
\text { (A) }\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+} & \text { (I) } 4.90 \\
\text { (B) }\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-} & \text { (II) } 3.87 \\
\text { (C) }\left[\mathrm{CoF}_6\right]^{3-} & \text { (III) } 0.0 \\
\text { (D) }\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_4\right]^{2-} & \text { (IV) } 2.83
\end{array}\)
Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** (A)-(II), (B)-(III), (C)-(I), (D)-(IV)
- **(B)** (A)-(II), (B)-(IV), (C)-(I), (D)-(III)
- **(C)** (A)-(I), (B)-(IV), (C)-(II), (D)-(III)
- **(D)** (A)-(IV), (B)-(III), (C)-(I), (D)-(II)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) (A)-(II), (B)-(IV), (C)-(I), (D)-(III)**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \text { (A) }\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right]^{3+} \\ & \mathrm{Cr}^{3+}: 3 \mathrm{~d}^3 \\ & \mathrm{n}=3 \text { (unpaired electrons) } \\ & \mu \simeq 3.87 \text { B.M. (II) } \\ & \end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & \text { (B) }\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-} \\ & \mathrm{Ni}^{2+}: 3 \mathrm{~d}^8 \\ & \mathrm{n}=2 \\ & \mu \simeq 2.83 \text { B.M. (IV) } \\ & \end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & \text { (C) }\left[\mathrm{CoF}_6\right]^{3-} \\ & \mathrm{Co}^{3+}: 3 \mathrm{~d}^6 \\ & \mathrm{n}=4 \\ & \mu \simeq 4.90 \text { B.M. (I) }\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & \text { (D) }\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_4\right]^{2-} \\ & \mathrm{Ni}^{2+}: 3 \mathrm{~d}^8 \\ & \mathrm{n}=0 \\ & \mu=0 \text { B.M. (III) } \\ & \end{aligned}$
SECTION -B

</details>

### Q40. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Number of complexes from the following with even number of unpaired " \(\mathrm{d}\) " electrons is \(\qquad\) \(\left[\mathrm{V}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{3+},\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+},\left[\mathrm{Fe}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{3+},\left[\mathrm{Ni}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{3+},\left[\mathrm{Cu}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+}\)
[Given atomic numbers : \(\mathrm{V}=23, \mathrm{Cr}=24, \mathrm{Fe}=26, \mathrm{Ni}=28 \mathrm{Cu}=29\)]

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2
- **(B)** 1
- **(C)** 4
- **(D)** 5

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & {\left[\mathrm{V}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{3+} \rightarrow \mathrm{d}^2 \mathrm{sp}^3} \\ & { }_{23} \mathrm{~V}:-[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^3 4 \mathrm{~s}^2 \\ & \left.\mathrm{~V}^{+3}:-[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^2, \mathrm{n}=2 \text { (even number of unpaired } \mathrm{e}^{-}\right) \\ & {\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+} \rightarrow \mathrm{sp}^3 \mathrm{~d}^2} \\ & { }_{24} \mathrm{Cr}:-[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^5 4 \mathrm{~s}^1 \\ & \left.\mathrm{Cr}^{+2}:-[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^4, \mathrm{n}=4 \text { (even number of unpaired } \mathrm{e}^{-}\right)\end{aligned}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/CWwl9lZ3I8UGU9rnw7gu7AZDjIBePwUq2GWrBvtInBY.original.fullsize.png">
$\begin{aligned} & {\left[\mathrm{Fe}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{3+} \rightarrow \mathrm{sp}^3 \mathrm{~d}^2} \\ & \mathrm{Fe}^{3+}:-[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^5 4 \mathrm{~s}^0 \\ & \mathrm{n}=5\left(\text { odd number of unpaired } \mathrm{e}^{-}\right) \\ & {\left[\mathrm{Ni}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{3+} \rightarrow \mathrm{sp}^3 \mathrm{~d}^2} \\ & \mathrm{Ni}:-[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^8 4 \mathrm{~s}^2 \\ & \left.\mathrm{Ni}^{+3}:-[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^7, \mathrm{n}=3 \text { (odd number of unpaired } \mathrm{e}^{-}\right) \\ & {\left[\mathrm{Cu}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)_6\right]^{2+} \rightarrow \mathrm{sp}^3 \mathrm{~d}^2} \\ & \mathrm{Cu}:-[\mathrm{Ar}] 3 \mathrm{~d}^9 4 \mathrm{~s}^0 \\ & \left.\mathrm{n}=1 \text { (odd number of unpaired } \mathrm{e}^{-}\right)\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q41. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Select the option with correct property:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup></math> both diamagnetic
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup></math> both paramagnetic
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup></math> diamagnetic, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> paramagnetic
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> diamagnetic, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup></math> paramagnetic

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> diamagnetic, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup></math> paramagnetic**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Properties of the diamagnetic materials are: The diamagnetic materials have all the paired electrons, and none of the electrons is the valence, resulting in the absence of atomic dipoles in these materials. This happens because the overall magnetic moment of each atom in the compound cancels out.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo></math> diamagnetic, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridization, number of unpaired electrons <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>If the substance contains unpaired electrons, it is a paramagnetic substance.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>−</mo></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>→</mo></math> paramagnetic, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridization, number of unpaired electrons <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q42. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The correct statements from the following are:</p><p>A. The strength of anionic ligands can be explained by crystal field theory.</p><p>B. Valence bond theory does not give a quantitative interpretation of kinetic stability of coordination compounds.</p><p>C. The hybridization involved in formation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo> </mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>–</mo></mrow></msup></math> complex is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.</p><p>D. The number of possible isomer(s) of cis-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>PtCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo> </mo><mo>(</mo><mi>en</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo> </mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is one</p><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, D only
- **(B)** A, C only
- **(C)** B, D only
- **(D)** B, C only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) B, C only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The strength of ligands discussed in spectrochemical series.</p><p>According to the limitations of VBT,</p><p>It does not give a quantitative interpretation of the thermodynamic or kinetic stabilities of coordination compounds.</p><p> Hybridisation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo> </mo><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.</p><p>The outer electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ion is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math>.</p><p>It is a square planar complex. Cyanide ion is a strong field ligand, thus all electrons will be paired.</p><p>One <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math>, one <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> and two <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mo> </mo></math>orbitals undergo <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridization, four equivalent <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybrid orbitals which are occupied by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> electron pairs of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> cyanide ligands.</p><p>This forms <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo> </mo><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cis</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>PtCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo> </mo><mo>(</mo><mi>en</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo> </mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> complex exhibits optical isomerism, hence, it has two isomers.</p><p>Hence statement B and C are correct.</p>

</details>

### Q43. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The molecule/ion with square pyramidal shape is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>PCl</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>BrF</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>PF</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>BrF</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>BrF</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math> is an interhalogen compound, meaning that the molecules of this compound contain two or more different halogen atoms but no atoms of the elements belonging to any other group.</p><p>The hybridisation of bromine pentafluoride forms an <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridisation, resulting in a square pyramidal molecular structure. The hybrid molecule has five sigma bonds due to the covalent electron sharing.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9f29cc21-dcdb-4ee9-ba85-f2d5aec7455f.png" style="width: 280px; height: 297px;"/></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q44. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Aluminium chloride in acidified aqueous solution forms an ion having geometry

**Options:**

- **(A)** Octahedral
- **(B)** Square Planar
- **(C)** Tetrahedral
- **(D)** Trigonal bipyramidal

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Octahedral**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"></math>Aluminium Chloride in acidified aqueous solution forms octahedral <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Al</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ion.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AlCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msup><mover><mrow><mover><mo>→</mo><mtext>Acidified aqueous solution</mtext></mover><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Al</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow></mrow></mover><mrow></mrow></msup></math></p><p>In this complex the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> orbital of Al are involved and the hybridisation state of Al is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.</p><p>Hence, the answer is option A.</p>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The Spin only magnetic moment value of square planar complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Pt</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mi>Cl</mi></math> is _______ B.M. (Nearest integer)
( Given atomic number for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Pt</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>78</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Spin only magnetic moment can be calculated by the following formula
Magnetic moment =<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt></math>
Where, n is number of unpaired electrons.</p><p>In the given compound, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Pt</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup></mfenced></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8c55c035-9c7e-40cc-95e8-4d21a1a0d152.png" style="width: 600px; height: 364px;"/></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Pt</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridization and have no unpaired electrons..</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo></math> Magnetic moment</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>0</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q46. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Identify from the following species in which <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridization is shown by central atom:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>BrF</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Pt</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>Cl</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>SF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> hybridization</p><p>In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Co</mi></math> is in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math> oxidation state with the configuration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math>. In the presence of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> a strong ligand, the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> electrons pair up leaving two d-orbitals empty. Hence, the hybridization is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>forming an inner orbital octahedral complex. </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>BrF</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridization</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>PtCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridization</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>SF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridization</p>

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Consider the following complex ions</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>–</mo></mrow></msup></math>,  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>)</mo><msub><mo> </mo><mn>6</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>,  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>The correct order of the complex ions, according to their spin only magnetic moment values (in B.M.) is :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo> </mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo> </mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo> </mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>–</mo></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mo>:</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mo>:</mo><mo> </mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mo> </mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>: Weak field Ligand </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/34f71cd9-34dc-497d-8ddb-bb50343774d0.png" style="width: 280px; ### Q48. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following complexes will exhibit maximum attraction to an applied magnetic field?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>en</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Complex having maximum number of unpaired electrons will exhibit maximum attraction to applied magnetic field.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Zn</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>10</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>system</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>eg</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></math> unpaired electrons
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>7</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>system</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>eg</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn></math> unpaired electrons
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>en</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>system</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>eg</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></math> unpaired electrons
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>system</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>eg</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></math> unpaired electrons.</p><p>So Option D is correct.</p>

</details>
