### Q50. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The magnetic moment is measured in Bohr Magneton (BM). Spin only magnetic moment of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi></math> in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> complexes respectively is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math> in both
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>87</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math> and<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>732</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>732</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>89</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>732</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The spin only magnetic moment can be calculated as follows</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo></math>Number of unpaired electrons</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>High</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>spin</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>complex</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math>.unpaired electrons
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>7</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>Low</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>spin</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>complex</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>732</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q51. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The ratio of spin-only magnetic moment values  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mi>eff</mi></msub><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mi>eff</mi></msub><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is _______

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In case of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ion , the valence shell electronic configuration is same whether the ligand is strong field or weak field.</p><p>The spin only magnetic moment , <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mi>eff</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p>The valence shell electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>, hence, it has three unpaired electrons.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mtext>eff&#160;</mtext></msub></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>15</mn></msqrt><mtext>&#160;B.M.&#160;</mtext></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mtext>eff&#160;</mtext></msub></math>of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>15</mn></msqrt><mtext>&#160;B.M.&#160;</mtext></math></p><p>Ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q52. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The octahedral diamagnetic low spin complex among the following is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>In case of strong field ligand containing complexes, electrons get pair up against the Hund&#39;s rule to get required number of hybrid orbitals. Among the given complexes&#39; ammonia is a strong-filed ligand and other ligands are weak filed ligands. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> (inner orbital complex) with zero unpaired electrons. Hence, it is octahedral with diamagnetic character and low spin complex. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#8211;</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#8211;</mo></mrow></msup></math> are octahedral but having unpaired electrons.<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is not octahedral.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q53. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of spin only magnetic moments for the following complex ions is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>MnBr</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>MnBr</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>MnBr</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>MnBr</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>MnBr</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>MnBr</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is a tetrahedral complex. The electronic configuration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>is<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></msubsup></math> and it has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> unpaired electrons.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt><mtext>&#160;B.&#160;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is an octahedral complex and it is high spin complex. The electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></msubsup><msup><msub><mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi></msub><mn>2</mn></msup></math>, it has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> unpaired electrons</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>24</mn></msqrt><mtext>&#160;B.M.&#160;</mtext></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is an octahedral complex and it is low spin complex. The electronic configuration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></msubsup><msubsup><mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup></math> it has two unpaired electrons</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>8</mn></msqrt><mtext>&#160;B.M&#160;</mtext></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is an octahedral complex and it is low spin complex. The electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>5</mn></msubsup><msup><mi>eg</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math> and it has one unpaired electron.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mtext>&#160;B.M&#160;</mtext></math></p><p>Hence the correct answer is option <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math></p><p> </p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following complex is octahedral, diamagnetic and the most stable?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The coordination compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>[</mo><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></math>contains a cobalt(<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>III</mi></math>) ion, which has a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math> electronic configuration. The cyanide ligands in this complex are indeed strong field ligands, which means they will cause a large splitting of the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>orbitals. So here cobalt is in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math> state having <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math> configuration and also cyanide ligand which is a strong field ligand so, it is most stable and will have octahedral shape. The given compounds have the following nature:</p><p>(1) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></math> &ndash; Paramagnetic</p><p>(2) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> &ndash; Paramagnetic</p><p>(3) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>&ndash; Diamagnetic</p><p>(4) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> &ndash; Paramagnetic</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q55. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The spin only magnetic moment of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> complexes is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math> (Nearest integer)</p><p>(Given atomic number of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn></math>)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>A spin-only magnetic moment occurs when an electron rotates on its own axis and generates a magnetic field.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mi>BM</mi></math>
Here, n is the number of unpaired electrons.
The unit for a spin-only magnetic moment is the Bohr magneton (BM).</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close=""><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>5</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>91</mn><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Cobalt chloride when dissolved in water forms pink colored complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> which has octahedral geometry. This solution on treating with cone <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math> forms deep blue complex, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> which has a <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> geometry. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math>, respectively, are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Tetrahedral</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Octahedral</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Octahedral</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Tetrahedral</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Tetrahedral</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Cobalt chloride when dissolved in water forms pink coloured is<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> . This is due to the electronic transition of an electron from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub></math> energy level of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> complex. When excess of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math> is added to the solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is transformed into deep blue colour complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo></mrow></msup></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>&#9651;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mo>&#9651;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>&#9651;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mo>&#9651;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msub></math></p><p>Tetrahedral complexes have smaller crystal field splitting than octahedral complexes.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#10230;</mo><munder><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace></munder></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow><mi>Conc</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>HCl</mi></mrow></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Tetrahedral</mi></math></p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>Br</mi></math> is mixed with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> solution of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>Br</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>. The resulting solution is divided into two equal parts <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mfenced></math> and treated with excess <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> solution and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>BaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> solution respectively as shown below:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> Solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> solution (excess) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></math> Solution <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>BaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> solution (excess) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math>
The number of moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> respectively are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The number of moles of  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>Br</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> </p><p>The number of moles of   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>Br</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p>The total volume of the solution becomes two Litres after mixing.</p><p>Following precipitation reactions occur:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>Br</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>+</mo></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8594;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>AgBr</mi><mo>&#8595;</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mi>mol</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>excess</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>Br</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mo>+</mo></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>BaCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mo>&#8594;</mo></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>BaSO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#8595;</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mi>mol</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>excess</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>01</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p>

</details>

### Q58. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Match List I with List II:</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 400px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align: center;"><strong>List I
			(Complexes)</strong></td>			<td colspan="2" rowspan="1" style="text-align: center;"><strong>List II
			(Hybridisation)</strong></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(A)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></td>			<td>I</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(B)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>II</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(C)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>III</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(D)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>IV</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p> </p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A &ndash; II, B &ndash; I, C &ndash; III, D &ndash; IV
- **(B)** A &ndash; I, B &ndash; II, C &ndash; III, D &ndash; IV
- **(C)** A &ndash; II, B &ndash; I, C &ndash; IV, D &ndash; III
- **(D)** A &ndash; I, B&ndash; II, C &ndash; IV, D &ndash; III

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A &ndash; I, B&ndash; II, C &ndash; IV, D &ndash; III**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi></math> is in zero oxidation state in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></math>. So, the electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>. As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi></math> is a strong ligand, it pushes all the electrons in the 3d orbital, therefore the hybridisation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>O</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></math><sub> </sub>is sp<sup>3</sup> and it has tetrahedral geometry. It is diamagnetic due to the absence of unpaired electrons.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ion, oxidation state of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cu</mi></math> is +2 and its valence shell electronic configuration is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>9</mn></msup></math>.  So, there would be a rearrangement of electrons in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> because of the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> strong field ligand . And the last electron in the d-orbital would be out waiting for the N&#39;s electrons to fill up first. So, the 4 electron pairs from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> would be in one 3d, one 4s, &amp; two 4p orbitals and in the third place of 4p orbital, the e<sup>-</sup> from 3d would take place. So, you can say the hybridisation here would be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.</p><p class="Solution_html__KkUW2" style="border: 0px; margin: 0px; padding: 0px; text-align: start; -webkit-text-stroke-width: 0px;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ion, oxidation state of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi></math> is +2 and its valence shell electronic configuration is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math>. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is strong field ligand with complex. So, no pairing will be there. So, you can say the hybridisation here would be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>.</p><p class="Solution_html__KkUW2" style="border: 0px; margin: 0px; padding: 0px; text-align: start; -webkit-text-stroke-width: 0px;"> </p><p class="Solution_html__KkUW2" style="border: 0px; margin: 0px; padding: 0px; text-align: start; -webkit-text-stroke-width: 0px;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ion, oxidation state of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi></math> is +2 and its valence shell electronic configuration is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math>. There are 4 unpaired electrons in 3d orbital.  So, you can say the hybridisation here would be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.</p><p class="Solution_html__KkUW2" style="border: 0px; margin: 0px; padding: 0px; text-align: start; -webkit-text-stroke-width: 0px;">So, correct order of matching is given in the below table:</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 300px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td colspan="1" rowspan="1" style="text-align: center;"><strong>List I
			(Complexes)</strong></td>			<td colspan="1" rowspan="1" style="text-align: center;"><strong>List II
			(Hybridisation)</strong></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table>

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>How many of the following metal ions have similar value of spin only magnetic moment in gaseous state? ________</p><p>(Given: Atomic number<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>23</mn><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Cr</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>26</mn><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>28</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>From magnetic moment formula:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mi>BM</mi><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo></math> no. of unpaired electrons )</p><p>Ion           E.C.            n
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>7</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>Thus, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#38;</mo><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> have same value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub></math></p>

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The number of paramagnetic species from the following is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mtext>&#160;and&#160;</mtext><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9521a510-4356-4489-a3c9-de4fcaa245c2-9894521.PNG" style="width: 133px; height: 50px;" /></p><p>It is diamagnetic as  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>CN</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><none></none><mo>-</mo></mmultiscripts></math> is a strong field ligand and forces electron pairing.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>:</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>=</mo></math><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/814b0b15-0043-4cb3-ad63-9683b628e680-9894521_1.PNG" style="width: 123px; height: 50px;" /></p><p>It is diamagnetic as carbonyl group is a strong field ligand and forces electron pairing.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo></math> <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1b37efcf-424c-4b49-bdff-1ee472d39138-9894521_2.PNG" style="width: 134px; height: 50px;" /></p><p>It is paramagnetic as chloride group is a weak field ligand.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo></math>- <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/de0978a1-873b-4a86-9302-1835743e3e41-9894521_3.PNG" style="width: 132px; height: 50px;" /></p><p>It is diamagnetic as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi>CN</mi></math> is a  strong field ligand which causes pairing of electrons.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Here Copper ion has one unpaired electron, thus it is paramagnetic as it is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>9</mn></msup></math> configuration.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo></math> <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3eeb62b5-e27d-422b-b6b3-6c8814d5a5ba-9894521_4.PNG" style="width: 129px; height: 50px;" /></p><p>It is paramagnetic, here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is a strong field ligand</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo></math><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7536d29e-e5b1-4451-9d93-ca62fa8cce88-9894521_5.PNG" style="width: 151px; height: 60px;" /></p><p>It is paramagnetic, here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> , here a weak field ligand</p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q61. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The hybridization and magnetic behaviour of cobalt ion in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> complex, respectively is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> and diamagnetic
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and paramagnetic
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and diamagnetic
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> and paramagnetic

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and diamagnetic**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Co</mi></math> is in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math> oxidation state with the configuration <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math>. In the presence of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> a strong field ligand, the 3d electrons undergo pairing and  leaving two d-orbitals empty. Hence, the hybridisation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> which forms an inner orbital octahedral complex. The complex is diamagnetic as it has no unpaired electrons.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cc6d985b-aa87-4e2e-a4f0-4807ba6f95c8-9894226.png" style="width: 600px; height: 262px;" /></p><p>So, the correct option is C</p>

</details>

---

### Q62. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The d-electronic configuration of  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> in tetrahedral crystal field is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></msup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></msubsup></math>. The sum of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and number of unpaired electrons is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **7**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The oxidation state of Co in complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>Electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mi>o</mi></math> is  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>C</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><mn>27</mn><none></none></mmultiscripts><mi>o</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>7</mn></msup></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><msup><mi>o</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>7</mn></msup></math></p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/72c1e895-0f5e-44f9-90a0-9e9499549eea-e8d36d19-fe4b-448f-aa1d-019a46db3226-9884748.png" style="width: 190px; height: 47px;" /></p><p>Since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is a weak field ligand, so the given complex will have a tetrahedral geometry with following Crystal-field splitting</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5b287574-c041-4be0-9fa7-7357f5d2e9fd-image.png" style="width: 297px; height: 155px;" /></p><p> </p><p>So, value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo></math>(unpaired electrons) is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

---

### Q63. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Octahedral complexes of copper <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math> undergo structural distortion (Jahn-Teller). Which one of the given copper <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math> complexes will show the maximum structural distortion?
(en-ethylenediamine; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><mfenced><mi>en</mi></mfenced><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** cis-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>en</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** trans-<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>en</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>According to John teller any nonlinear molecular system in a degenerate electronic state will be unstable and will undergo some kind of distortion which will lower its symmetry and energy and split the degenerate state. In case of octahedral d9 configuration, the last electron may occupy either dz<sup>2</sup> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dx</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>orbitals of eg set. If it occupies dz<sup>2</sup> orbital most of the electron density will be concentrated between the metal and the two ligands on the z axis. Thus there will be greater electrostatic repulsion associated with these ligands than with the other four on xy plane. The Jahn Teller effect is mostly observed in octahedral environments. The considerable distortions are usually observed in high spin d<sup>4</sup>, low spin d<sup>7</sup> and d<sup>9</sup> configuration</p><p>There is unsymmetrical filling of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub></math> subset of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> ion, while there is symmetrical distribution in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math> set, if the complex has same ligand there will be equal repulsion which leads to symmetrical bond length along <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub></math>, but due to uneven filling of electron in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub></math> subset, either octahedral will be elongated or compressed.</p><p>Hence,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo></math>maximum structural distortion</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q64. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Match List-I with List-II</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><strong>List-I
			(Complex)</strong></td>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><strong>List-II
			(Hybridization)</strong></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>A</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></math></td>			<td>I</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>B</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>II</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>C</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>III</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>D</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>IV</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></td>		</tr>	</tbody></table><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>II</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi></math> is in zero oxidation state in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>4</mn></msub></math> so the electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>. As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi></math> is a strong ligand, it pushes all the electrons in the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math> orbital, therefore the hybridisation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math> and it has tetrahedral geometry. It is diamagnetic due to the absence of unpaired electrons.</p><p>In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>4</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>, there is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> ion for which the electronic configuration in the valence shell is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math>.</p><p>In presence of strong field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> ions, all the electrons are paired up.</p><p>The empty, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> and two <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math> Orbitals undergo <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridization to make bonds with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> ligands in square planar geometry.</p><p>The atomic number of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Co</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn></math> and its valence shell electronic configuration is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>7</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Co</mi></math> is in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math> oxidation state in the complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><msub><mi>CoF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> hybridized and it is octahedral in shape.</p><p>Another <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> complex, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>[</mo><mi>Co</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>6</mn></msub><msup><mo>]</mo><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>, is diamagnetic and has no unpaired electrons. The hybrid orbitals used to form this complex are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></math> Hybridisation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> Hybridisation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> Hybridisation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>Sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> Hybridisation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q65. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> cation gives a prussian blue precipitate on addition of potassium ferrocyanide solution due to the formation of

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>OH</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Ferric ion forms a prussian blue precipitate due to the formation of <math><mi>F</mi><msub><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></math> . In this method, to a portion of sodium fusion extract, freshly prepared ferrous sulphate <math><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>S</mi><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math> solution is added and warmed. Then about <math><mn>2</mn></math> to <math><mn>3</mn></math> drops of <math><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></math> solution are added and acidified with conc. <math><mi>H</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi></math> . The appearance of a prussian blue colour indicates the presence of nitrogen.

<math><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>S</mi><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>O</mi><mi>H</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>H</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>N</mi><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>S</mi><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>6</mn><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>N</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><msub> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>O</mi><mi>H</mi></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo>&#8594;</mo><munder> <mrow> <mi>N</mi><msub> <mi>a</mi> <mn>4</mn> </msub> <mrow><mo>[</mo> <mrow> <mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><msub> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>C</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> <mn>6</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mo>]</mo></mrow></mrow> <mrow> <mtext>Sodium</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>ferrocyanide</mtext></mrow> </munder> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>O</mi><mi>H</mi></mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mi>N</mi><msub> <mi>a</mi> <mn>4</mn> </msub> <mrow><mo>[</mo> <mrow> <mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><msub> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>C</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> <mn>6</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mo>]</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>C</mi><msub> <mi>l</mi> <mn>3</mn> </msub> <mo>&#8594;</mo><munder> <mrow> <mi>F</mi><msub> <mi>e</mi> <mn>4</mn> </msub> <msub> <mrow> <mrow><mo>[</mo> <mrow> <mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><msub> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>C</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> <mn>6</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mo>]</mo></mrow></mrow> <mn>3</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mtable columnalign="left"> <mtr> <mtd> <mrow> <mtext>Ferric&#160;ferrocyanide</mtext></mrow> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mtext>prussian</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>blue</mtext></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> </munder> <mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></math>
