### Q66. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The conductivity of a solution of complex with formula <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></math> corresponds to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math> electrolyte, then the primary valency of central metal ion is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Primary valencies are ionizable. If the complex ion exhibits positive charge, then primary valence corresponds to the number of charges present and is balanced by the same number of negative ions. Hence, primary valency can also be defined by the number of anions neutralizing the charge on the complex. </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>Cl</mi></math></p><p>The above complex can be represented as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo></msup><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>. Hence, the primary valency of above complex is 3.</p></blockquote>

</details>

- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>FeCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced></math>

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Oxidation number of of copper is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Electronic configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cu</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>10</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p>Due to the absence of unpaired electrons, their spin only magnetic moment is zero, they are diamagnetic in nature</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cu</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>9</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></math> one unpaired electron is present.Hence, paramagnetic in nature.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced></math>:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>one</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>unpaired</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>electron</mi></math>.Hence, paramagnetic in nature.</p><p> </p></blockquote>

xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ar</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>3</mn><msup><mi **Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>MnBr</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></math></p><p>Number of unpaired electrons <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>Magnetic moment of the complex,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>91</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>.</p><p>5.91 is rounded off to the nearest integer 6.</p><p> </p>

### Q71. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Ti</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Among the given complexes, number of paramagnetic complexes is</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Complexes in which central atom has unpaired electrons is said to be paramagnetic in nature and where unpaired electrons are not present are diamagnetic in nature.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo></math>,   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow></mrow></msubsup><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mn>1</mn></msup></math>, paramagnetic (one unpaired electron)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><msup><mi ### Q72. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

<p>Arrange the following coordination compounds in the increasing order of magnetic moments. (Atomic numbers: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>;</mo><mi>Fe</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn></math>)</p><p>A. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>B. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>C. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>MnCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> (high spin)</p><p>D. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Magnetic moment is directly proportional to the number of unpaired in the central metal atom of a complex.</p><p>It is calculated by the formula, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mi>BM</mi></math></p><p>(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeF</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/04627242-343e-4dcd-9292-6947c756fcb9-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 94px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>MnCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/166f1f71-9c4d-407a-8b9e-3af526584d61-image.png" style="width: 140px; height: 100px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/579613fb-9b3e-47ed-aae7-ad64d4fc34ac-image.png" style="width: 120px; height: 73px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mn</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hence</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>correct</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>order</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>magnetic</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>moment</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>is</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math></p>

</details>

<p>(I) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>PtCl</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo></math> no ppt with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Pt</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>CN</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p>(II) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>excess</mi></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></math> mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCl</mi><mo>&#8595;</mo></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>CN</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>8</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>82</mn><mi>BM</mi><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>Aqua complexes, generally have <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CN</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p>(III) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>Cl</mi><munder><mover><mo>&#10230;</mo><msub><mi>AgNO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mover><mi>excess</mi></munder><mn>1</mn></math> Mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCl</mi><mo>&#8595;</mo></math>,</p><p><math - **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>III</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>IV</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mi>II</mi></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;">	<tbody>		<tr>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><strong>Compound</strong></td>			<td style="text-align: center;"> </td>			<td style="text-align: center;"><strong>Compound</strong></td>			<td style="text-align: center;">Structure</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(A)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>PtCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>(I)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></td>			<td>Square planer</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(B)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>BrF</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math></td>			<td>(II)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></td>			<td>Square pyramidal</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(C)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>PCl</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></math></td>			<td>(II)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></math></td>			<td>trigonal pyramidal</td>		</tr>		<tr>			<td>(D)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></td>			<td>(IV)</td>			<td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></td>			<td>octahedral</td>		</tr>	</tbody></table>

### Q76. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Spin only magnetic moment in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>BM</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>92</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn></math>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo></msup></math></p><p>Oxidation no. of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math> </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mrow></mrow><mn>26</mn></msub><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>Ar</mi><mn>18</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mn>5</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msup></math> </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi></math> is a Strong field ligand, so paring occurs against the Hund&#39;s rule. </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>eg</mi><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></msup></math> </p><p>Number of unpaired electron <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> </p><p>so magnetic moment <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math> B.M. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p>

</details>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math> moles of metal complex with formula <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>en</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> gives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math> moles of silver chloride on treatment with excess of silver nitrate. <summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

### Q78. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Three moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCl</mi></math> get precipitated when one mole of an octahedral co-ordination compound with empirical formula <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CrCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> reacts with excess of silver nitrate. The number of chloride ions satisfying the secondary valency of the metal ion is ______ .

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **0**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AgCl</mi></math> precipitated is equal the mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> present in ionization sphere.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/475592a4-b141-4c66-9e20-3cf474972004-image.png" style="width: 374px; height: 160px;" /></p><p>Since none of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is present in the co ordination sphere. Therefore answer is zero.</p>

</details>

- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>46</mn></math>

therefore</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1d7e681a-b951-4fd6-889e-8d49cee51cab-image.png" style="width: 288px; height: 184px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> No of unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mi>BM</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>[<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo></math> No of unpaired <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></math>]</p><p> </p>

### Q82. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The spin-only magnetic moment value for the complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is...... <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>BM</mi></math>. (nearest integer value)
[At. no. of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Co</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>27</mn></math>]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><menclose notation="box"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></menclose></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>:</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mn>7</mn></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>CN</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> is a strong field ligand which can pair electron of central atom.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8c71d052-cc91-4423-a2b8-264248dddc2d-500af322-836d-4703-bebb-9659baac96c4-image.png" style="width: 420px; height: 199px;" /></p><p>It has one unpaired electron (n) in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo></math>subshell. So spin only magnetic moment <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>
where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo></math> number of unpaired electrons.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>73</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8771;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BM</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

According to the valence bond theory the hybridization of central metal atom is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>dsp</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> for which one of the following compounds?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
- **(B)** <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>1) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><msub><mo>]</mo><mn>18</mn></msub><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mn>8</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dab3c1ac-d7c3-4cab-9eac-a91d2b5b5492-c2be5f44-3acd-4596-8ac7-89ebc2e3b31e-image.png" style="width: 390px; height: 141px;" /></p><p>2) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></math>    <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ni</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><msub><mo>]</mo><mn>18</mn></msub><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mn>8</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/32b7c705-1e1a-4bf1-b4b1-aaf73e336203-image.png" style="width: 407px; height: 260px;" /></p><p>3) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
CO<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo></math>Strong field ligand</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/05b919cf-7d43-4091-be8f-7988b7627399-image.png" style="width: 377px; height: 125px;" /></p><p>4) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>NiCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ni</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mo>[</mo><mi>Ar</mi><msub><mo>]</mo><mn>18</mn></msub><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mn>8</mn></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msup></math>
CN=4, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Hybridisation</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>sp</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></p>

### Q84. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The total number of unpaired electrons present in the complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><mo>(</mo><mtext>oxalate</mtext><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> is______.

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

Chromium is in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math> oxidation state.</p><p>It has stable <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow/></msubsup><mo>&#160;</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>   configuration</p><p>Number of unpaired electrons in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math> will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p>

### Q85. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>On complete reaction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>FeCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> with oxalic acid in aqueous solution containing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>KOH</mi></math>, resulted in the formation of product <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>. The secondary valency of Fe in the product A is ______ .</p><p>(Round off to the Nearest Integer).</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><munder><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></munder></math></p><p>Secondary valency of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi></math> in &#39;A&#39; is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(a) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mmultiscripts><mi>Ce</mi><mprescripts/><mn>58</mn><none/></mmultiscripts><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mi>Xe</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>5</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>6</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>In complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ce</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mi>Xe</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p>there is no unpaired electron so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>(b) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mmultiscripts><mi>Gd</mi><mprescripts/><mn>64</mn><none/></mmultiscripts><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mi>Xe</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mn>7</mn></msup><mn>5</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>6</mn><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p>contain seven unpaired electrons so, <math mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>(c) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mmultiscripts><mi>Eu</mi><mprescripts/><mn>63</mn><none/></mmultiscripts><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mmultiscripts><mi>Xe</mi><mprescripts/><mn>54</mn><none/></mmultiscripts></mfenced><mn>4</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mn>5</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mn>6</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>0</mn></msup></math></p><p>contain six unpaired electron </p><p>so, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>6</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>48</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Hence, order of spin only magnetic movement </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math></p>

### Q87. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The equivalents of ethylene diamine required to replace the neutral ligands from the coordination sphere of the trans-complex of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is _______ . (Round off to the Nearest Integer).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>1. trans- <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
or</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>trans</mo></math>- <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi>&#8467;</mi></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d1feef5c-fa49-4551-8edd-72bc780f2cab-image.png" style="width: 243px; height: 391px;" /></p><p>As we know that ethylene diamine is a bidentate ligand and ammonia is a mono dentate ligand.</p><p>It means overall two ethylene diamine is required to replace the all neutral ligands (four ammonia) from the coordination sphere of this complex.</p>

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The pair in which both the species have the same magnetic moment (spin only) is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Co</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>OH</mi></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8211;</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Mn</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>CoCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8211;</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

### Q89. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Complex A has a composition of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>12</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>C</mi><mi>r</mi></math>. If the complex on treatment with conc. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> loses <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>13</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>%</mo></math> of its original mass, the correct molecular formulas of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is:
[Given : atomic mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>52</mn></math> amu and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>35</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></math>amu]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close=""><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><mfenced close="]"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#183;</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi></mrow></mfenced><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Conc. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> acts as dehydrating agent.</p><p>Molar mass of given complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>266</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>On treating with conc. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> the mass lost by the complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>13</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>100</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>266</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8776;</mo><mn>36</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p><p>Formula of the complex <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Cr</mi><msub><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q90. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The oxidation states of iron atoms in compounds <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></math>, respectively, are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></math>. Then sum of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></math> is ........... <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><munder><mrow><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mfenced><mi>NOS</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mfenced></munder><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><munder><mrow><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced open="[" close="]"><msub><mi>FeO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced></mrow><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced></munder><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><munder><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>9</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mfenced></munder></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Oxidation state of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi></math> in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>Fe</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>CN</mi></mfenced><mn>5</mn></msub><mfenced><mi>NOS</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math>:</p><p>Charge of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CN</mi></math> =<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Charge of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NOS</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Suppose oxidation state of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>Oxidation state of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>in</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced close="]" open="["><msub><mi>FeO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced></math>:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>Oxidation state of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>in</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mfenced><mi>CO</mi></mfenced><mn>9</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> because carbonyl is neutral ligand.</p><p>The oxidation states of iron in these compounds will be</p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></math><p>The sum of oxidation states will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>6</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q91. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of the spin-only magnetic moments of the following complexes is:
<math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math> <math><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>
<math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>)</mo></math> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mo>)</mo></math> <math><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>≈</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&gt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

As <math><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>n</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>
Complex (II) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#10233;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#10233;</mo><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,2</mo><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi>eg</mi><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,0</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#10233;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
Complex (I) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Cr</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,1</mo><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi>eg</mi><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,0</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>24</mn></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>
Complex (IV) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Co</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">W</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,2</mo><mo>,</mo></mrow></msubsup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mo>^</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,1</mo><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>15</mn></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>
Complex (III) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8594;</mo><msup><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></msub><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,1</mo><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi>eg</mi><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,0</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math>

</details>

### Q92. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The major product <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mfenced></math> in the following sequence of reactions is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7de1d4ad-8d74-431d-954f-740e9a342a3a.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dba73362-8b6f-4335-843f-f82b5461f0b5.png">
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/26eb4f44-95c2-4534-8933-ed6abc7be0fd.png" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1474ade3-f2f5-4adb-97f3-2510185b9fb9.png">
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/41ed51c8-8c7b-4df0-8c9d-756efc0cc436.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/26eb4f44-95c2-4534-8933-ed6abc7be0fd.png" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/11QJ.PNG" style="width: 400px; height: 300px;" /></p><p>Alcohol upon reaction with protic acids tends to lose a molecule of water to form alkenes. These reactions are known as dehydrogenation or dehydration of alcohols. It is an example of an elimination reaction.The order of the ease of dehydration of alcohols is: tertiary &gt; secondary &gt; primary. </p>

</details>

### Q93. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Among the statements <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mfenced></math> , the incorrect ones are:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mfenced></math> Octahedral <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> complexes with strong field ligands have very high magnetic moments
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mfenced></math> When <math><msub><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>P</mi></math> , the d-electron configuration of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> in an octahedral complex is <math><msubsup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msubsup><msubsup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfenced></math> Wavelength of light absorbed by <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is lower than that of <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mfenced></math> If the <math><msub><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math> for an octahedral complex of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is <math><mn>18,000</mn><mi></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, the <math><msub><mrow><mo>&#8710;</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub></math> for its tetrahedral complex with the same ligand will be <math><mn>16,000</mn><mi></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> only
- **(B)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> only
- **(C)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> only
- **(D)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/838f1e93-45c2-49d8-b0f5-c3daeda284d0-image.png" style="width: 278px; height: 298px;" />
So if there are strong field ligands in complex, pairing of electrons will take place, due to which number of unpaired electrons decrease due to which magnetic moment decrease.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mo>)</mo></math> Splitting power of ethylenediamine (en) is greater than fluoride <math> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>F</mtext> <mo>&#8722;</mo> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </math> ligand therefore more energy absorbed by <math> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <mtext>Co</mtext><mfenced> <mrow> <mtext>en</mtext></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow>&#160;&#160;</msup></mrow></math> as compared to  <math><mrow><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><msub><mrow><mtext>CoF</mtext></mrow> <mtext>6</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>

So wave length of light absorbed by <math> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <mtext>Co</mtext><mfenced> <mrow> <mtext>en</mtext></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> is lower than that of <math> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mtext>CoF</mtext></mrow> <mtext>6</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>(d)&#160;</mtext><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>so&#160;if&#160;&#160;</mtext><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>18</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mtext>cm</mtext><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mtext>t</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>18000</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>8000</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mtext>cm</mtext><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

Statement <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>)</mo></math> are incorrect.

</details>

### Q94. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct statements among I to III are: 
(I) Valence bond theory cannot explain the color exhibited by transition metal complexes. 
(II) Valence bond theory can predict quantitatively the magnetic properties of transition metal complexes. 
(III) Valence bond theory cannot distinguish ligands as weak and strong field ones.

**Options:**

- **(A)** (I) and (II) only
- **(B)** (II) and (III) only
- **(C)** (I) and (III) only
- **(D)** (I), (II) and (III)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) (I) and (II) only**

**Detailed Solution:**

Valence Bond Theory cannot explain the colour of transition metal complexes .this theory was also unable to distinguish between weak and strong Fields ligands. Valence Bond Theory does not explain and give quantitative interpretation of magnetic data. Hence, statement (II) is not right.

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The degenerate orbitals of <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mrow></msub></math>.
- **(B)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow></msub></math>.
- **(C)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msub></math>.
- **(D)** <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow></msub></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow></msub></math>.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Degenerate orbitals of <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mtext>Cr</mtext><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mtext>H</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext>2</mtext></mrow></msub><mtext>O</mtext></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mtext>6</mtext></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mtext>3+</mtext></mrow></msup></math>: </p><p>Orbitals having same energy call degenerate orbital, And here water acts as weak ligands which split <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo></math>orbital into two parts.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dd45e518-c4e3-4574-9119-b7f13c75f68f-image13.png" />
Hence, according to the options given, degenerate orbitals are <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow></msub></math>. </p>

</details>

### Q96. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The calculated spin-only magnetic moments <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> of the anionic and cationic species of <math> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <mtext>Fe</mtext><msub> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mn>6</mn> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mtext>Fe&#160;</mtext><msub><mfenced><mtext>CN</mtext></mfenced><mn>6</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> respectively, are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>0</mn></math> and <math><mn>4.9</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2.84</mn></math> and <math><mn>5.92</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>4.9</mn></math> and <math><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>0</mn></math> and <math><mn>5.92</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>4.9</mn></math> and <math><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In both cases iron exhibits <math><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math> oxidation state.</p><p>Electronic configuration of <math> <mrow> <mi>F</mi><msup> <mi>e</mi> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> ion <math><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>]</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d0ff0037-0834-43b5-a25f-39410f5565ff-image41.png" style="width: 260px; height: 55px;" /></p><p>In <math><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> is a weak field ligand and hence, no pairing up.</p><p><math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> number of unpaired electron <math><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math>Spin magnetic moment <math><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>24</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>4.9</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math></p><p>In <math><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is a strong field ligand and hence, pairing will occur.</p><p><math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> number of unpaired electron <math><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> spin magnetic moment = zero</p>

</details>
