### Q97. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of the spin &ndash;only magnetic moment of metal ions in the following low-spin complexes, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><msup><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

(i) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>23</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#10233;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#10233;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e19f6dc7-31eb-4a0e-a432-a0c9a9a96a60-image33.png" /> <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> <math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><msqrt><mn>3</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>3.87</mn></math>
(ii) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>24</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#10233;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup></math> <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/67561494-ead5-4563-8881-d06857765c46-image34.png" /> <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> <math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2.82</mn></math>
(iii) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>44</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#10233;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></math>   <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/333e097a-5b51-43d0-b0c7-ae84515e489a-image35.png" /> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math> <math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>
(iv) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>26</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#10233;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi></math> <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8189e6c7-dd9b-439a-97eb-fbd63e65345e-image36.png" /> <math><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo></math> <math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
Order of magnetic moment will be <math><mo>&#8733;</mo></math> no. of unpaired <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
<math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></msqrt></math> B.M. Hence more the value of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math> more will be magnitude moment.

</details>

### Q98. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The magnetic moment of an octahedral homoleptic <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mn</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>)</mo></math> complex is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.9</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>.</mo></math> . The suitable ligand for this complex is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>C</mi><mi>O</mi></math>
- **(C)** Ethylenediamine
- **(D)** <math><mi>N</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>S</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mi>N</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>S</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>M</mi><msup><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> will have configuration <math><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5.93</mn><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mi>n</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math>

or, <math><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math> as <math><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>5.93</mn></math>

If has <math><mn>5</mn></math> unpaired electron which are not getting paired up. This implies that the ligand paired up, and the ligand is a weak field ligand. Among the given options, weak ligand is <math><mi>N</mi><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>S</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q99. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The metal&#39;s <math><mi>d</mi><mo>-</mo></math> orbitals that are directly facing the ligands in <math><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></msub></math>  and  <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msub></math>  and  <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></msub></math>  and  <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>y</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></msub></math>  and  <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msub></math>  and  <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Give <math><msub><mrow><mi>K</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>[</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>o</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub><mo>]</mo></math>  is inner orbital complex with hybridization  <math><msup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>s</mi><msup><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>  and octahedral geometry. Ligands are approaching metal along the axes. Hence  <math><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msub></math>  orbitals are directly in front of the ligands.

</details>

### Q100. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The pair of metal ions that can give a spin only magnetic moment of <math><mn>3.9</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>B</mi><mi>M</mi></math> for the complex <math><mo>[</mo><mi>M</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub><mo>]</mo><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> , is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mi>F</mi><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mi>F</mi><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>  and  <math><mi>M</mi><msup><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><msup><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>If the complex is <math><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>M</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><msub><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>M</mi></math> will have <math><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math> oxidation state and hence, it has already lost <math><mn>2</mn></math> electrons from <math><mi>s</mi><mo>-</mo></math> orbital. Since it has <math><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3.9</mn></math> <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><msqrt> <mrow> <mi>n</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> </msqrt> <mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow> </math>

<math><mn>3</mn></math> unpaired so, the configuration can either be:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5d1c60d6-1754-44d0-a3de-0ce0e954e394-image.png" style="width: 290px; height: 126px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q101. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The major product of the following reaction is,

<img height="60" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/844490c0-771c-4c9f-bcdc-1fb1209de735-image.png" width="244" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RCOOH</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RCHO</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>RCH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>RCONH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RCHO</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In the first step, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RCN</mi></math> gets reduced to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RCH</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>NH</mi></math>, which then gets hydrolyzed to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>RCH</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>.</p><p><img height="74" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/61ff28a4-b461-417e-96a2-43e370ce80a2-image.png" width="506" /></p>

</details>

### Q102. JEE Main 2018 (16 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In Wilkinson's catalyst, the hybridization of central metal ion and its shape are respectively:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>s</mi><msup><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi>d</mi></math>, trigonal bipyramidal
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>s</mi><msup><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>, octahedral
- **(C)** <math><mi>d</mi><mi>s</mi><msup><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, square planar
- **(D)** <math><mi>s</mi><msup><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></math>, tetrahedral

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mi>d</mi><mi>s</mi><msup><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, square planar**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Wilkinson catalyst

<math><mo>[</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>h</mi><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>P</mi><msub><mrow><mi>h</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>]</mo></math>

Metal with 4d<sup>8</sup> and 5d<sup>8</sup> Configuration always forms  square planar complexes on account of higher CFSE irrespective of weak field or strong field. The magnetic property is diamagnetic.


<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5080fe3a-0b9e-474b-a85a-8301685de95c-image.png" style="width: 350px; height: 250px;" /></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q103. JEE Main 2018 (16 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

In a complexometric titration of metal ion with ligand <math><mi>M</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>L</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>)</mo></math> end point is estimated spectrophotometrically (through light absorption). If &#39;M&#39; and &#39;C&#39; do not absorb light and only &#39;L&#39; absorbs then the titration plot between absorbed light <math><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>)</mo></math> versus volume of ligand &#39;L&#39; <math><mo>(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>)</mo></math> would look like:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fd8851ce-de89-441d-a331-e5f1de188677-image.png" style="width: 249px; height: 235px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b3c656a9-513c-4d51-94fb-6c2011308cd2-image.png" style="width: 266px; height: 251px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1e8761e8-6364-40a0-9278-1c5b804bb2af-image.png" style="width: 271px; height: 267px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ba87516d-5c0b-4988-8097-a0772c33b4e7-image.png" style="width: 268px; height: 268px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fd8851ce-de89-441d-a331-e5f1de188677-image.png" style="width: 249px; height: 235px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

Initially ligand consumed by metal due to formation of complex. So absorbed light  <math><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>)</mo></math>  remain constant, after complex formation is completed, extra volume of ligand solution increases ligand concentration and also increases absorbed light.

</details>

The correct combination is

- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>CN</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo></math> tetrahedral; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Ni</mi><msub><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>CO</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo></math> paramagnetic

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

### Q105. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr Shift 1 Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct combination is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}$ - square-planar; $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_4\right]^{2-}$ - paramagnetic
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_4\right]^{2-}$-tetrahedral; $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]$ - paramagnetic
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}$- paramagnetic; $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]$ - tetrahedral
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}$- dimagnetic; $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]$ -square-planar

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}$- paramagnetic; $\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]$ - tetrahedral**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CN})_4\right]^{2-}$ is square planar, diamagnetic $(0$ unpaired electrons) with $d s p^2$ hybridisation. 
$\left[\mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{CO})_4\right]$ - is tetrahedral,diamagnetic ( $0$ unpaired electrons) with $s p^3$ hybridisation. 
$\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}$ is tetrahedral, paramagnetic ($2$ unpaired electrons) with $s p^3$ hybridisation. 
Hence, the option (c) is the correct answer.

</details>

### Q106. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The pair having the same magnetic moment is:

<math><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>24</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>27</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><msup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The compounds having the same number of unpaired electrons will have same magnetic moment.</p><p>Magnetic moment <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>&#956;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msqrt><mi>n</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt></math></p><p>where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> is the number of unpaired electrons</p><p><math> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mrow><mo>[</mo> <mrow> <mtext>C</mtext><mtext>r</mtext><msub> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow> </mrow> <mn>6</mn> </msub> </mrow> <mo>]</mo></mrow> </mrow> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>C</mtext><msup> <mtext>r</mtext> <mo>2+</mo> </msup> <mo>.</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>3</mn><msup> <mtext>d</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msup> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext> </mrow> </math> with weak field ligand so unpaired electrons are 4<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/33333333ddddd.JPG" style="width: 150px; height: 49px;" /></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msqrt><mn>24</mn></msqrt></math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>F</mtext><msup> <mtext>e</mtext> <mrow> <mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>3</mn><msup> <mtext>d</mtext> <mn>6</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow> </mrow> </math> Weak field ligand so unpaired electrons are 4<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/333333dddd6666.JPG" style="width: 150px; height: 47px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msqrt><mn>24</mn></msqrt></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q108. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following is diamagnetic ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{ox})_3\right]^{3-}$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{FeF}_6\right]^{3-}$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{CoF}_6\right]^{3-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\left[\mathrm{Co}(\mathrm{ox})_3\right]^{3-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\left[\mathrm{Fe}(\mathrm{CN})_6\right]^{3-}$ has magnetic moment of a single unpaired electron whereas $\left[\mathrm{FeF}_6\right]^{3-}$ has a magnetic moment of five unpaired electrons. $\left[\mathrm{CoF}_6\right]^{3-}$ is paramagnetic with four unpaired electrons while $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{O}_4\right)_3\right]^3$ is diamagnetic. This anomaly is explained by valence bond theory in terms of formation of inner and outer orbital coordination entities. $\left[\mathrm{Co}\left(\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{O}_4\right)_3\right]^{3-}$ is an inner orbital complexes having $d^2 s p^3$ hybridization.

</details>

### Q110. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of ligands in the spectrochemical series is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}>$en $>\mathrm{CN}^{-}>\mathrm{NCS}^{-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{CN}^{-}>$en $>\mathrm{NCS}^{-}>\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{NCS}^{-}>\mathrm{CN}^{-}>\mathrm{Cl}^{-}>$en
- **(D)** en $>\mathrm{CN}^{-}>\mathrm{Cl}^{-}>\mathrm{NCS}^{-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{CN}^{-}>$en $>\mathrm{NCS}^{-}>\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Correct order is : $\mathrm{CN}^{-}>$en $>\mathrm{NCS}^{-}>\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$

</details>

### Q111. JEE Main 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following facts about the complex $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right] \mathrm{Cl}_3$ s wrong ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** The complex is paramagnetic
- **(B)** The complex is an outer orbital complex
- **(C)** The complex gives white precipitate with silver nitrate solution
- **(D)** The complex involves $d^2 s p^3$ hybridization and is octahedral in shape.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) The complex is an outer orbital complex**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_6\right] \mathrm{Cl}_3$ involves $\mathrm{d}^2 \mathrm{sp}^3$ hybridization and it is an inner orbital complex.

</details>

### Q112. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which one of the following has a square planar geometry?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{CoCl}_4\right]^{2-}$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{FeCl}_4\right]^{2-}$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{NiCl}_4\right]^{2-}$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{PtCl}_4\right]^{2-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\left[\mathrm{PtCl}_4\right]^{2-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

${ }_{27} \mathrm{Co}^{2+}$-$\mathrm{1 s^2 2 s^2 2 p^6 3 s^2 3 p^6 3 d^7 4 s^0}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/csmv0aLUZ6zvcCFUJ6ywCClYEHuiL2OKIvPWHQE8Mc0.original.fullsize.png">

As $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$is weak field ligand so no pairing up. 
Hence it is $sp^3$ hybridized giving tetrahedral geometry. 
$\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}$-$\mathrm{1 s^2 2 s^2 2 p^6 3 s^2 3 p^6 3 d^6 4 s^0}$<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/-bpCciP00OmAFAUiOvz_e05K-riAR5kEMdSGw3eC1C0.original.fullsize.png">

Due to $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$, back pairing is not observed so it will be $\mathrm{sp}^3$ hybridized giving tetrahedral geometry.
$\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}-1 \mathrm{s}^2 2 \mathrm{s}^2 2 \mathrm{p}^6 3 \mathrm{s}^2 3 p^6 3 \mathrm{d}^8 4 \mathrm{s}^0$<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/gjwFrYt0BRFy96Yo500-s-woeeloDggSVf3gDBtZNDI.original.fullsize.png">

Because weak ligand, back pairing is not observed so it will be $\mathrm{sp}^3$ i.e. tetrahedral geometry. 
All the complexes of $\mathrm{Pt}^{2+}$ are square planar including those with weak field ligand such as halide ions thus (D) is correct.

</details>

### Q114. JEE Main 2006

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Nickel $(Z=28)$ combines with a uninegative monodentate ligand $\mathrm{X}^{-}$to form a paramagnetic complex $\left[\mathrm{NiX}_4\right]^{2-}$. The number of unpaired electron(s) in the nickel and geometry of this complex ion are, respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** one, tetrahedral
- **(B)** two, tetrahedral
- **(C)** one, square planar
- **(D)** two, square planar

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) two, tetrahedral**

**Detailed Solution:**

${ }_{28} \mathrm{Ni}: \ldots \ldots \ldots \mathrm{s}^2, 3 \mathrm{p}^6, 3 \mathrm{~d}^8, 4 \mathrm{~s}^2$
$\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}: 3 \mathrm{~s}^2, 3 \mathrm{p}^6, 3 \mathrm{~d}^8$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/gIxoVrBUUIRO0L169d1shPS0u35QDIEHPKhlhopXeuM.original.fullsize.png">

</details>

### Q116. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The oxidation state of $\mathrm{Cr}$ in $\left[\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4 \mathrm{Cl}_2\right]^{+}$is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+3$
- **(B)** $+2$
- **(C)** $+1$
- **(D)** 0

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $+3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\left(\mathrm{Cr}\left(\mathrm{NH}_3\right)_4 \mathrm{Cl}_2\right)^{+}$
$X+4 \times 0+2 \times-1=1$
$\mathrm{X}=+3$

</details>
